# List of military aid to Ukraine during the Russo-Ukrainian war

Main article: [Military aid to Ukraine during the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War)

**Countries delivering military aid to Ukraine**

&#x20; Delivered heavy weapons to Ukraine

&#x20; Delivered non-material military aid to Ukraine

&#x20; Ukraine

Many entities have provided or promised [military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_aid) to [Ukraine](31750.md) during the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), particularly since the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md). This includes [weaponry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapon), [equipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_equipment_of_the_Armed_Forces_of_Ukraine), [training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_education_and_training), [logistical support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_logistics), as well as [financial support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aid), unless earmarked for [humanitarian purposes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarianism). Weapons sent as a result of cooperation between multiple countries are listed separately under each country.

The aid has mostly been coordinated through the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group), whose 57 member countries include all 32 [member states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_NATO) of [NATO](21133.md). The [European Union](9317.md) [co-ordinated weapons supplies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_logistics) through [its institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutions_of_the_European_Union) for the first time.[^cite_note-\:93-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3] The Russian invasion led several donor countries, including [Germany](11867.md) and [Sweden](5058739.md), to overturn their national policies on providing offensive [military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_aid).

By March 2024, the majority of [Western](21208200.md) governments had pledged more than \$380 billion worth of aid to Ukraine since the invasion, including nearly \$118 billion in direct military aid from individual countries.[^cite_note-4] As of December 2024, European countries have provided €132 billion in aid (military, financial, and humanitarian), and the [United States](3434750.md) has [provided](70376708.md) €114 billion.[^cite_note-\:81-5] Most of the US funding supports American industries that produce weapons and military equipment.[^cite_note-cfr.org-6]

Fearing escalation, NATO states have hesitated to provide heavier and more advanced weapons to Ukraine or have imposed limits such as forbidding Ukraine from using them to strike inside Russia.[^cite_note-7] Since June 2024, they have lifted some of these restrictions, allowing Ukraine to strike Russian military targets near the border in self-defence.[^cite_note-8]

According to defence expert Malcolm Chalmers, at the beginning of 2025, the US provided 20% of all military equipment Ukraine was using, with 25% supplied by Europe and 55% produced by Ukraine. However, the 20% provided by the US "is the most lethal and important."[^cite_note-9]

&#x20;\


## Donors

The donation of military aid was coordinated at monthly meetings in the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) throughout the war. A [first meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Ramstein_Air_Base_meeting) took place between 41 countries on 26 April 2022, and the coalition comprised 54 countries (all 30 [member states of NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_NATO) and 24 other countries) at the latest meeting on 14 February 2023.[^cite_note-UDCG_2023-10] All [EU member states](10890716.md) collectively donated military aid via EU institutions; all but three ([Hungary](13275.md), [Cyprus](5593.md), and [Malta](19137.md)) also donated individually as sovereign countries.

As of February 2023, military aid was donated by [EU institutions](#European_Union), [45 sovereign countries](#Sovereign_countries), [companies](#Companies), and [other parties](#Other_parties).

| [Colombia](5222.md) | On 23 May 2022, Colombian Defence Minister [Diego Molano Aponte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Molano_Aponte) announced that a team of 11 [demining](2206580.md) engineers of the [National Army of Colombia](2697243.md) will travel to an unspecified NATO country to train their Ukrainian counterparts on landmine removal.[^cite_note-149] |
| [Croatia](5573.md) | The [Croatian Minister of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Croatia\)), [Mario Banožić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Bano%C5%BEi%C4%87), approved military and humanitarian aid for Ukraine on 28 February 2022. As of November 2025, Croatia has announced the delivery of 15 military aid packages to Ukraine, with 14 packages of military aid worth over \$212 million being fully delivered.[^cite_note-150] On October 27, 2025, Croatian Defence Minister [Ivan Anušić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Anu%C5%A1i%C4%87) and Ukrainian Defence Minister [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal) signed a Letter of Intent to expand joint arms production and deepen defence-industrial ties between the two countries.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152] **Transport aircraft** As of June and July 2024, Croatia is preparing to supply a total of 2 [An-32B](908204.md) military transport aircraft to the [State Emergency Service of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Emergency_Service_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154] **Helicopters** 14 [Mi-8](957233.md) helicopters (including Mi-8MTV-1 variants) \[Nine delivered in May 2023, further five in June 2023].[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-Oryx-Croatia-156] **Armoured fighting vehicles** 30 [M-84](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M-84) tanks.\[^cite_note-\:25-157] Their appearance on the Ukrainian battlefield was publicly confirmed for the first time in September 2025.[^cite_note-158] 30 [BVP M-80](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BVP_M-80) \[to be delivered].\[^cite_note-\:25-157] **Artillery** 15 130mm [M-46](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/130_mm_towed_field_gun_M1954_\(M-46\)) (M-46H1 variant) field guns \[13 August 2022].[^cite_note-159] 40 [D-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/122_mm_howitzer_2A18_\(D-30\)) howitzers \[before March 2023].[^cite_note-slobodna-bosna.ba-160] | [Czech Republic](5321.md) | As of the beginning of February 2023, the Czech Republic donated equipment worth CZK 10 billion (\~ €420 million) from the [Czech Army stock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_equipment_of_the_Czech_Army) or purchased from private Czech companies.[^cite_note-FialaInfoFeb23-168] During the first year after the full-scale Russian invasion, Czech arms companies delivered to Ukraine military equipment worth CZK 30 billion (\~ €1,27 billion), either through direct purchase by Ukraine or by other countries supporting Ukraine.[^cite_note-FialaInfoFeb23-168] By February 2024, the Czech Republic approved commercial military exports to Ukraine worth CZK 130 billion (\~ €5,13 billion).[^cite_note-169] 676 pieces of heavy equipment were delivered from the Czech Republic to Ukraine by July 2023,[^cite_note-zelinprg-170] and this number rose to 834 by mid-April 2024[^cite_note-Hudson_speech-171] and 918 by the end of May 2024[^cite_note-CZ_May24-172] **Training and rehabilitation** *Before 24 February 2022* 1,200 Ukrainian soldiers received training in the Czech Republic [''Vyšpoint'' Military Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/''Vy%C5%A1point''_Military_Academy?action=edit\&redlink=1) by a combined Czech-British team of instructors (The British Military Advisory Training Team based in the Czech Republic), with particular focus on training of non-commissioned officers[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] *After 24 February 2022* [Czech Republic](5321.md)[European Union](9317.md) [EUMAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Military_Assistance_Mission_in_support_of_Ukraine) five-week training courses were provided primarily by the [7th Mechanized Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7th_Mechanized_Brigade_\(Czech_Republic\)). 2023: 3.240 Ukrainian soldiers trained in the Czech Republic. Initially, mostly mechanized infantry (+ logistics, CBRN, army engineers).[^cite_note-CZ_2-24_update-175][^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179][^cite_note-ct24.ceskatelevize.cz-180] From October 2023, light infantry training with a focus on trench warfare[^cite_note-ct24.ceskatelevize.cz-180] 2024: Target of \~ 4.000 Ukrainian soldiers to be trained by the Czech Army[^cite_note-CZ_2-24_update-175] [Czech Republic](5321.md)[European | [France](5843419.md)[^cite_note-Oryx2-314]\[^cite_note-\:69-315] | France was the largest arms exporter to Ukraine between 2014 and 2020, providing over €1.6 billion worth of weapons and military equipment.[^cite_note-316] Among them were: helicopters, reconnaissance drones, navy-related weaponry and equipment, targeting systems, ammunition of various calibres, and fire control systems.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] The full extent of French military aid in the context of the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine is unknown, as many specifics are neither shared by France nor Ukraine, but include: Defence equipment donated before the Russian invasion and pre-war training of Ukrainian troops.[^cite_note-lopinion.fr10-319] Additional delivery of defence equipment to the Ukrainian authorities was announced on 26 February 2022.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-aljazeera.com2-321] On 13 April 2022, the French Minister of Defence announced the provision of additional weapons to Ukraine.[^cite_note-bfmtv.com3-322] On 30 April 2022, President Macron pledged an increase in military support to Ukraine,[^cite_note-323] reiterating on 17 May 2022 that weapon deliveries would not only continue but would additionally increase in the days and weeks to come.[^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325] [Lecornu](54364868.md) also added the supply of batteries of [Exocet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exocet) anti-ship missiles to help open gaps in the maritime blockade imposed by Russia and allow Ukraine to resume its exports of grain and raw materials, which was among the further weapons deliveries being examined.[^cite_note-rtl.fr2-326][^cite_note-327] French contribution to the European Peace Facility Fund (a €500 million contribution out of a total of €3 billion as of December 2022).[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329] On 13 October 2022, the creation of a French special military fund, totaling €200 million, was inked. It will allow Ukraine to purchase military equipment it desires from French defence contractors.[^cite_note-LeParisien-2022-330][^cite_note-331] On 15 October 2022, it was announced that France would be training up to 2,000 Ukrainian soldiers on its soil. They will be assigned to French units for several weeks.[^cite_note-LeParisien-2022-330][^cite_note-332] On 4 January 2023, President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) promised President [Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zelenskyy) [AMX-10 RC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMX-10_RC) and [ACMAT Bastion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACMAT_Bastion) to Ukraine. This is the first time Ukraine has received "Western tanks," according to the French government.[^cite_note-333] On 31 January 2023, it was announced that France will also send 150 military personnel to Poland to train up to 600 Ukrainian troops per month.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] **Fighter jets** An unspecified number of [Dassault Mirage 2000-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Mirage_2000) and pilot training \[delivered from February 2025 onwards].[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] [MICA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MICA_\(missile\)) air-to-air missiles for Mirage jets, more delivered September 2026\[^cite_note-\:44-338]\[^cite_note-\:99-339] 30mm ammunition.\[^cite_note-\:99-339] **Intelligence, surveillance, and reconnaissance support** Intelligence, surveillance, and reconnaissance support, including ongoing access to French satellite imagery (various French electromagnetic and observation satellites, such as the [CERES](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CERES_\(satellite\)), [Composante Spatiale Optique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Composante_Spatiale_Optique), [Pléiades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pl%C3%A9iades_\(satellite\)), and [Helios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios_2_\(satellite\)), provide intel several times a day to the Ukrainian military staff)[^cite_note-lopinion.fr10-319] and a few dozen agents from the [Directorate General for External Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directorate_General_for_External_Security)'s [Action Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Division) deployed on the ground since the beginning of the war.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] **Cruise missiles and aerial bombs** [SCALP EGs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCALP_EG) \[Since July 2023]. 10 additional SCALP EGs were pledged late 2024.\[^cite_note-\:28-342] 50 [AASM Hammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armement_Air-Sol_Modulaire) aerial bombs per month \[delivered from March 2024 onwards][^cite_note-343] 63 [Mark 81 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_81_bomb) **Air defence systems** A [SAMP/T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAMP/T) battery (joint donation with Italy) \[May 2023].[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] Two additional SAMP/T batteries on loan to Ukraine, to be returned upon delivery of purchased batteries.[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347] Additional munitions delivered September 2026.\[^cite_note-\:99-339][^cite_note-348] Two [Crotale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crotale_\(missile\)) NG batteries \[November 2022]. Acquisition of two complete short-range air defence systems from Thales, including [Ground Master 200](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_Master_200) radars, command and control centre, radio communications system, and air threat designation terminals (purchased through the French military fund) \[One delivered in 2023, another one ordered in 2024][^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] [Aster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aster_\(missile_family\)) air defence missiles.[^cite_note-351] **Multiple rocket launchers** Four 227mm | [Serbia](29265.md) | Despite Serbia officially maintaining a policy of military neutrality and publicly denying direct arms supplies to Ukraine since Russia's full-scale invasion in February 2022, multiple intelligence reports, leaked documents, and investigative journalists' investigations revealed that Serbia has, since the beginning of Russia's 2022 invasion of Ukraine, supplied large quantities of Serbian-made ammunition and related military equipment to Ukraine.[^cite_note-828] Serbia's transfers of weapons and ammunition to Ukraine have often involved intermediaries, falsified end-user certificates, and re-exports from NATO allies and third-party countries, including Bulgaria, Czechia, Poland, Slovakia, Spain, Turkey, and the United States.[^cite_note-829] **Ammunition** Several million ammunition rounds of [7.62 mm caliber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7.62_mm_caliber) and [5.45×39mm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5.45%C3%9739mm) cartridges, which are compatible with AK-series rifles.[^cite_note-830] An unspecified amount of MAM-L smart micro munitions, which the Ukrainian Army has been using in Turkish-made Bayraktar TB2 combat drones.[^cite_note-831] **Artillery shells** Hundreds of thousands of different types of artillery shells, including [high-explosive incendiary/armour-piercing ammunition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-explosive_incendiary/armor-piercing_ammunition) and artillery shell components that could be assembled abroad. Some of the artillery shells that have been provided in the largest quantities have been shells for 122mm/155mm [wheeled self-propelled howitzers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-propelled_artillery).[^cite_note-832] **Mortars** Between 20.000 and 30.000 rounds of anti-personnel mines and high-explosive mortar rounds.[^cite_note-833] **Rockets** The Serbian defence manufacturing company [EDePro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EDePro) produced thousands of G-2000 122mm Grad rockets, which were subsequently delivered to Ukraine via third parties, where they have been used in the [Grad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-21_Grad) [multiple launch rocket systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_rocket_launcher).[^cite_note-834] |

| [South Korea](27019.md) | **Currently, South Korea maintains its stance not to supply Ukraine with lethal weapons.** **Vehicles** Two K600 mine clearing vehicles to Ukraine for "humanitarian" missions \[to be delivered].[^cite_note-OryxSouthKorea-861][^cite_note-862] 60 pickup trucks donated to Ukrainian National Police.[^cite_note-863] **Military gear[^cite_note-OryxSouthKorea-861]** Helmets \[delivered since March 2022]. Bulletproof Vests \[before July 2023]. Military Uniforms \[delivered since March 2022]. Mine Detectors \[before July 2023]. Explosive Ordnance Disposal (EOD) Suits \[before July 2023]. Gas Masks \[before July 2023]. **Miscellaneous items** \[delivered since March 2022][^cite_note-OryxSouthKorea-861] Meal, Ready-to-Eat (MREs). First-Aid Kits. Tents. Blankets. **Relevant information** South Korea lent 550,000 rounds of [M107 155 mm artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M107_projectile) to the United States in 2022 and 2023, which the shells were originally produced by the United States and brought to South Korea between 1974 and 1978 as WRSA-K (War Reserve Stockpile for Allies–Korea) then transferred the ownership to South Korea. Since South Korea law prohibits sale or transfer of lethal weapons to Ukraine, the shells filled the U.S. inventory while the United States supplied Ukraine from its own inventory. The United States requires to sign a contract with South Korean company for recompense, and [South Korean military](27027.md) seeks to receive the state-of-art ammunition instead of old M107.[^cite_note-864][^cite_note-KorReutersArty-865][^cite_note-KorWsjArty-866] South Korea provided 100 million KRW worth of non-lethal military supplies such as bulletproof vests, helmets, gas masks, medical supplies, ready-to-eat (MREs), etc., which were originally to be delivered to the [military of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Afghanistan). Delivered via [NATO](21133.md) in March 2022.[^cite_note-867][^cite_note-868][^cite_note-869] As of July 2023, South Korea sent a total of 4.85 billion KRW worth of non-lethal supplies in four occasions, with portable mine detectors and bomb proof suits included in the 4th shipment.[^cite_note-870] South Korea donated US\$100 million (as of Dec 2022) to international organization for humanitarian aid in Ukraine.[^cite_note-871] Bilateral [official development assistance](1316305.md) to be increased by designating Ukraine as key 중점협력국 *"Priority Cooperation Country"* for years 2021–2025.[^cite_note-MOFA-872][^cite_note-YonhapCite-873] |

22,000 Ukrainian troops as part of [Operation Orbital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Orbital) \[since 2015 to 2021].[^cite_note-998] This operation was suspended following the full-scale Russian invasion, a new British-led multinational operation commenced on 9 July 2022 as part of [Operation Interflex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Interflex). 17 June 2022 the UK offered to set up [Operation Interflex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Interflex) a program to administer and provide three weeks general infantry, first aid, cyber security, and counter explosive tactics training to 10,000 Ukrainian soldiers every four months,[^cite_note-999] this would better equip Ukraine to replace battlefield casualties. The program commenced on 9 July 2022[^cite_note-politico1-1000] ultimately 10,000 Ukrainian troops passed through training during 2022, with an additional 20,000 to be trained in 2023.[^cite_note-1001] Over 68,000 Ukrainians trained by mid 2026.[^cite_note-1002] 8 February 2023, training program expanded to include conversion training of Ukrainian fighter pilots to NATO fighter jets and a training program for Marines.[^cite_note-1003] Training Ukrainian sailors from May 2022.[^cite_note-GOV.UK-2023-978] 10,000 Ukrainian soldiers to be trained in the first half of 2024 as part of Operation Interflex.[^cite_note-GOV.UK-2024b-984] 200 Ukrainian aviators in the United Kingdom have completed basic flying, ground and English language training before further F-16 training by the end of 2024.[^cite_note-1004] Training for Ukrainian helicopter pilots.[^cite_note-1005] **Intelligence** Deployment of [RC-135W Rivet Joint surveillance aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_RC-135) to provide information on size and position of Russian forces.[^cite_note-1006] ISTAR (intelligence, surveillance, target acquisition, and reconnaissance) support, both standalone and in partnership with the United States.[^cite_note-1007] **Financial aid** 23 February 2022 - pledged £3.5 billion in British export financing, underwrote \$500 million in [MLDB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilateral_Development_Banks) borrowing and provided a £100 million loan via the World Bank for economic development.[^cite_note-1008] 3 March 2022- UK donated \$100 million directly to the Ukrainian government budget to mitigate financial pressures created by Russia's unprovoked and illegal invasion.[^cite_note-1009] 24 March 2022- £25 million in financial backing for the Ukrainian military.[^cite_note-1010] 9 April 2022- UK increased its World Bank loan guarantees to £730 million (US\$1 billion).[^cite_note-gov.uk-1011] 25 April 2022- UK announced it was cutting tariffs and quotas on all trade with Ukraine to zero.[^cite_note-1012] 4 July 2022- During the Ukraine Recovery Conference in Lugano, Switzerland the UK committed to fiscal grants through the World Bank of £99m and to underwriting £429m (\$525m) in a third tranche of World Bank lending.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-1013] 19 August 2022 the UK committed £1.5m for the testing of grain sold by Russia to identify if it had come from Ukraine, and a package of rail support for grain exports.[^cite_note-UK_commits_millions_to_helping_the-1014] 13 April 2023 the UK pledged an additional \$500m (£400m) of World Bank loan guarantees taking its total financial support pledged in 2023 to \$1bn and £6.5bn of military/financial aid provided in total so far.[^cite_note-1015] Over £80 million in funding for the NATO Comprehensive Assistance Package for Ukraine since February 2022.[^cite_note-1016] £245 million in funding to procure and invigorate supply chains to produce artillery shells for Ukraine announced February 2024.[^cite_note-1017] On 23 April 2024, British PM [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak) announced a new military aid package for Ukraine. Which included "60 boats with offshore raiding craft and dive boats; more than 1,600 strike and air defence missiles; more than 400 vehicles, including 160 protected mobility "Husky" vehicles and 162 armoured vehicles; as well as nearly 4m rounds of small arms ammunition." plus £500m in funding. The largest funding package the UK has put together for Ukraine.[^cite_note-1018] On 22 October 2024 The British government announced a £2.26 billion loan to Ukraine for military spending, to be repaid with revenue generated by frozen Russian assets.[^cite_note-1019] £120 million donation to the Maritime Capability Coalition October 2024.[^cite_note-1020] £7.5 million of funding for new attack and surveillance drones for Ukraine through the Drone Coalition November 2024.\[^cite_note-\:292-405] A £40 million economic recovery programme for unlocking millions of pounds in private lending to increase growth and trade January 2025.[^cite_note-1021] A £2.26 billion loan to Ukraine to finance the domestic production of weapons to be repaid with revenue generated by frozen Russian assets, all tranches delivered by April 2026.[^cite_note-1022][^cite_note-1023] £250 million funding for the purchase of American made weapons for Ukraine pledged February 2025.\[^cite_note-\:86-975]\[^cite_note-\:97-990] £40 million million funding Project Renovator providing wounded Ukrainian soldiers with medical and rehabilitation services, including five medical rehabilitation wards.[^cite_note-1024][^cite_note-1025] €300 million contribution to deliver JAS 39 Gripen aircraft to Ukraine.[^cite_note-1026] |

### European Union

See also: [European Union Military Assistance Mission Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Military_Assistance_Mission_Ukraine)

Individual [EU member states](10890716.md) have provided military, financial, and non-lethal material aid since 2014. The following list is the aid collectively provided by the EU. Most of this aid has been coordinated by the [European Commission](9974.md).

- Around €17 billion in grants and loans from 2014 until 12 February 2022.

  [^cite_note-1066]

- €1.2 billion loan approved 16 February 2022

  [^cite_note-1067]

- €450 million worth of lethal weapons, announced on 27 February 2022,

  [^cite_note-1068]

  &#x20;under the [European Peace Facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Peace_Facility).

- €50 million worth of non-lethal aid, announced on 27 February 2022.

  [^cite_note-1069]

- Provision of satellite intelligence, notably through the [European Union Satellite Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Satellite_Centre), as part of 1 March 2022 resolution on the Russian aggression against Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-1070]

- Increase of military aid under the European Peace Facility to €1 billion, announced on 23 March 2022.

  [^cite_note-1071]

- Military aid increased to €1.5 billion under the European Peace Facility on 13 April 2022, assistance includes personal protective equipment, first aid kits and fuel, as well as military equipment.

  [^cite_note-1072]

- Protective gear worth over €977,000 donated to the [State Border Guard Service of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Border_Guard_Service_of_Ukraine) on 8 July 2022.

  [^cite_note-1073]

- Ten cars filled with humanitarian aid and 2 prisoner escort vans donated to the State Border Guard Service by [EULEX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EULEX).

  [^cite_note-1074]

- An additional €500 million military aid package for Ukraine approved by the EU on 18 July 2022.

  [^cite_note-1075]

- Around €500,000 worth of thermal imaging equipment provided by the EU for the State Border Guard Service of Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-1076]

- Two trucks and 11 off-road vehicles purchased for the State Border Guard Service of Ukraine through a grant by [Frontex](3072700.md).

  [^cite_note-1077]

- €225 million in funding for the training of the Ukrainian Armed Forces from the European Peace Facility as part of [EUMAM Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EUMAM_Ukraine).

  [^cite_note-1078]

- Training for over 80,000 Ukrainian soldiers by EUMAM.

  [^cite_note-1079]

- €200,000 of medical equipment donated to three Ukrainian Border Guard Service Hospitals 8 February 2024.

  [^cite_note-1080]

- 12 mine detection dogs supplied to the Armed Forces of Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-1081]

- €130,000 worth of equipment including explosive disposal kits, solar powered surveillance cameras, communications equipment and first aid kits donated to the State Border Guard Service of Ukraine 12 September 2024.

  [^cite_note-1082]

- 60 4x4 SUVs delivered to the [Ukrainian National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Guard_of_Ukraine) financed by [the Netherlands](21148.md).

  [^cite_note-1083]

- 25 [Volkswagen Amarok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_Amarok) pick up trucks donated to the National Police of Ukraine co-financed by Germany.

  [^cite_note-1084]

- Two trucks and 5 pickup trucks provided to the Ukrainian Border Guard Service April 2025.

  [^cite_note-1085]

- €18.1 billion in macro financial assistance as part of the G7 Extraordinary Revenue Acceleration loans initiative to be paid back with interest on frozen Russian assets in 2025.

  [^cite_note-1086]

- €6.5 million of EW systems, 4X4s and minibuses for the police and Border Guard, co financed with Germany.

  [^cite_note-1087]

- Five mini excavators donated to the Ukrainian Border Guard.

  [^cite_note-1088]

### Iranian-smuggled weapons

The following list attempts to provide an overview of Iranian-made or Iranian-smuggled weapons in use by the Ukrainian armed forces. They are believed to be intercepted armaments originally supplied by Iran intended for Yemen [Houthi rebels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_rebels). France and the United States had seized these weapons under [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2216](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2216).[^cite_note-1096][^cite_note-1097] The US government announced that it has donated to Ukraine over 1 million rounds of 7.62 mm ammunition,[^cite_note-1098] thousands of rocket-propelled grenade proximity fuses and thousands of pounds of propellant for rocket-propelled grenades seized from ships used by Iran through [civil forfeiture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_forfeiture_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-Bertrand-2023-1099] The US government is seeking to turn over additional seizures of thousands of rifles, hundreds of machine guns and rocket launchers and dozens of anti-tank guided missiles to Ukraine.[^cite_note-Bertrand-2023-1099] On 4 April 2024 the United States government transferred over 5,000 AK-47s, machine guns, sniper rifles, RPG-7s and over 500,000 rounds of 7.62mm ammunition to the Ukrainian armed forces.[^cite_note-DOJ-2024-1100]

**Heavy mortars**

- 120mm [HM-16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HM_16) (First sighted July 2022)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

**Light mortars**

- 82mm HM-19 (First sighted May 2022)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

**Small arms**

- [Type 56-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_56_assault_rifle) assault rifle (First sighted April 2022)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- AK-47 (April 2024)

  [^cite_note-DOJ-2024-1100]

- [RPG-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RPG-7) (April 2024)

  [^cite_note-DOJ-2024-1100]

**Ammunition**

- 80mm S-8OF HE-FRAG rockets for B-8 rocket pod (First sighted March 2023)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- 122mm OF-462 artillery rounds for [D-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/122_mm_howitzer_2A18_\(D-30\)) howitzer (First sighted September 2022)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- 122mm HE-FRAG rockets for BM-21 'Grad' MRL (First sighted January 2023)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- 152 mm artillery rounds for D-20 howitzer (First sighted September 2022)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- 125mm OF19 tank rounds (First sighted February 2023)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- 120mm M48 mortar rounds for HM-16 mortar (First sighted March 2023)

  [^cite_note-Oryx-9-1101]

- 7.62 mm ammunition (April 2024)

  [^cite_note-DOJ-2024-1100]

- 200 Prusa MK4S 3D printers with filament and spare parts.

  [^cite_note-1180]

  [^cite_note-1181]

- By 24 July 2022, a Polish crowdfunding campaign on the crowdfunding website Zrzutka raised over Zł 23,035,000 to purchase a [Baykar Bayraktar TB2](62445596.md) for the Ukrainian military,

  [^cite_note-1182]

  [^cite_note-1183]

  &#x20;the drone was subsequently given to Poland by Baykar Tech free of charge, with the €5 million collected used for aid.

  [^cite_note-Oryx-1184]

- Revolver 860 Armed VTOL unmanned aerial vehicle \[March, April or May 2022] (A few sold to Polish company. Subsequently transferred to Ukraine).

  [^cite_note-1185]

- The [Estonian Voluntary Rescue Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonian_Voluntary_Rescue_Association) donated a MV-4 robotic mine clearing vehicle to Ukraine on 29 August 2023.

  [^cite_note-1186]

- [Hampshire and Isle of Wight Air Ambulance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampshire_and_Isle_of_Wight_Air_Ambulance) has donated medical supplies worth up to £5,000 to the Ukrainian military.

  [^cite_note-1189]

  [^cite_note-1190]

  &#x20;An ambulance, a truck, a car, two boats, a trailer and heating equipment were donated November 2024.

  [^cite_note-1191]

- 50 Latvian made drones crowdfunded by the Latvian public media campaign "In Thoughts and Deeds with Ukraine August 2024.

  [^cite_note-1192]

- One [Steyr-Puch Pinzgauer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steyr-Puch_Pinzgauer) 6x6 armoured vehicle donated to the [46th Airmobile Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/46th_Airmobile_Brigade_\(Ukraine\)) by [Motorsport UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport_UK).

  [^cite_note-1193]

- 23 ambulances donated to the Ukrainian military by the New Zealand charity KIWI K.A.R.E.

  [^cite_note-1194]

- 4,320 ballistic vests, 1,580 helmets, seven 50-bed field hospitals donated by The [California National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_National_Guard) June 2022.

  [^cite_note-1195]

  [^cite_note-1196]

  [^cite_note-1197]

- 15 Liebherr truck mounted cranes, 2 tracked mulcher vehicles, hundreds of search lights and tens of thousands of engineering tools to Ukrainian State Border Guard engineering units by the German Federal Police.

  [^cite_note-1198]

- Five Ford transit vans, a speedboat and a Vector reconnaissance drone provided to the [10th Mobile Border Detachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10th_Mobile_Border_Detachment) by German Federal Police.

  [^cite_note-1199]

- 27,017 FPV drones funded for the Ukrainian Armed Forces by the Czech group Drony Nemesis.

  [^cite_note-1200]

  [^cite_note-1201]

- 10 Hazard loitering munitions, a control station and a [HMMVW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMMVW) donated to the [GRU](1406269.md) by the American group Help Heroes of Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-1202]

- 11 vehicles, consisting of snowmobiles, motorbikes and all terrain vehicles donated to Ukrainian Border Guards by Latvian, Polish and Austrian volunteers.

  [^cite_note-1203]

- 100 FPV drones donated to the Ukrainian army and police by the Latvian charity foundation Ziedot.lv

  [^cite_note-1204]

- 11 vehicles including an armoured Land Rover, trucks and ambulances donated to the Ukrainian military by the British group Driving Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-1205]

  [^cite_note-1206]

  [^cite_note-1207]

- €397,169 crowd funded by Czech and Slovak volunteers to purchase 254 122mm artillery shells from the Slovak arms manufacturer MSM group for the Ukrainian military.

  [^cite_note-1208]

- Four buses, three ambulances an SUV, a generator, defibrillators, turnstiles and medical equipment donated to Ukrainian State Border Guard Service by Santa Clara Monastery Foundation.

  [^cite_note-1209]

  [^cite_note-1210]

- 500 tons of improvised anti drone netting delivered by to the Ukrainian military by Danish volunteers.

  [^cite_note-1211]

  [^cite_note-1212]

- Night vision equipment, tablets, 120 FPV drones and a Hummer SUV donated to the GRU by the Latvian group Atbalsta Biedrība.

  [^cite_note-1213]

  [^cite_note-1216]

  [^cite_note-1217]
